I... I came in because I can't shake this certainty that I'm being punished. It's about my father. I let him decline, and now this is my punishment. My sister, she tries to tell me otherwise. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah... I guess sad is one word for it. It's more like... most days I wake up with this heavy, hollow feeling. Like the color has just gone out of everything. I can sit at my desk in HR, or stare out at my garden beds at home, and it just feels like none of it reaches me anymore. It's all just blank. 

Speaking of the garden, my hydrangeas are completely dying. I tried putting coffee grounds in the soil—my neighbor, Mrs. Gable, swore by it. She has these massive, bright blue blooms every summer. She told me the acidity is the trick, but honestly, I think she just has better soil. Her husband used to be a landscaper before he retired. They have this gorgeous stone retaining wall he built himself. It took him two summers to finish it because he insisted on sourcing the stones from this specific quarry upstate. I remember they had a flat tire on the way back from the quarry once, and they had to wait four hours for a tow truck because it was a holiday weekend. Oh, I hate driving on holiday weekends. The traffic on the interstate is just a nightmare. Last Labor Day, I was stuck behind a semi-truck for forty miles... 

Wait, what was I saying? Oh, right. The hollow feeling. Yeah, it's just... it's there all the time. Like I'm just empty inside.

Guilty? Yes. I... I keep going over every single decision I made about his appointments. The dummy they use for CPR is so creepy, with those blank eyes...

It's been... gosh, about a year and two months now. It all started when my father's health really took a turn and his behavior started changing, and I suddenly had to step in and do all the caregiving.

Pleasure? No... no, not really. I used to love working in my garden, like I said, but now I just look at it and I don't feel anything. I used to read a lot, too. Mostly historical fiction. I had this whole collection of books about the Tudor dynasty.

judgment has already been passed on me.\n- key: S020\n  name: Hallucinations\n  description: Perception-like experiences that occur without an external stimulus and are as vivid and clear as normal perception.\n  manifestation: When the house is quiet at night, I sometimes hear a woman's voice as clearly as if she's in the hallway saying, 'You failed him,' and I've gotten up more than once to check even though no one is there.\n- key: S024\n  name: Depressed_Mood\n  description: A state where a sad, empty, or hopeless mood persists.\n  manifestation: Most days I wake up with this heavy, hollow feeling, like the color has gone out of everything, and I can sit at my desk in HR or stare at the garden beds and feel like none of it reaches me anymore.\n- key: S027\n  name: Sleep_Disturbance\n  description: A state where there is a significant change in the quantity or quality of sleep.\n  manifestation: I fall asleep late after replaying phone calls and care decisions, then I'm awake around 3 or 4 in the morning with my chest tight, unable to get back to sleep before work.\n- key: S028\n  name: Psychomotor_Changes\n  description: Observable increase or decrease in psychomotor activity.\n  manifestation: People at work have started asking if I'm sick because I move so slowly through routine tasks, and sometimes it takes me a noticeable moment to answer even simple questions in meetings.\n- key: S030\n  name: Worthlessness_Guilt\n  description: Feelings of worthlessness or excessive or inappropriate guilt (which may be delusional; not merely self-reproach or guilt about being sick).\n  manifestation: I keep going over every decision about my father's appointments and every tense conversation with my relatives, and I end up feeling like I'm a selfish daughter and a useless employee who deserves whatever happens.\n- key: S032\n  name: Death_Suicide\n  description: Recurrent thoughts of death, recurrent suicidal ideation, or a suicide attempt.\n  manifestation: I don't have a plan to hurt myself, but I catch myself thinking it would be easier if I didn't wake up, especially on mornings when I know there are more calls from the care agency and problems waiting at work.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: delusions_and_hallucinations occur exclusively during the major depressive episode\n  manifestation: I never heard voices or believed I was being punished before this depression settled in around my father's decline; those experiences only started after months of feeling low, guilty, and worn down.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  manifestation: I've missed deadlines, avoided difficult employee meetings I normally handle, and stopped showing up for library volunteering because I can't keep myself steady enough to follow through.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 1 year and 2 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on for about a year and two months, since my father's health and behavior started changing and I had to step into caregiving.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 46 / human_resources_worker / bachelors\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S019\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty about what happened with your father?\n\nPatient: Guilty? Yes. I... I keep going over every single decision I made about his appointments. Every time I had to reschedule because of work, every tense conversation with my relatives about who was going to check on him. I just end up feeling like I'm this incredibly selfish daughter. And a useless employee, too, honestly. I feel like I just deserve whatever happens to me now. \n\nIt's funny how those conversations with relatives go, isn't it? My Aunt Susan, she always had an opinion about everything, but she never actually offered to drive him. She lives in this massive house out in the suburbs, the kind with the wrap-around porch. She had it painted this pale yellow color last spring. The painters left a mess on her driveway, though—paint chips everywhere. She spent three weeks fighting with the contractor about getting it power-washed. She's always fighting with someone. Last year it was the homeowner's association because they said her mailbox was the wrong shade of white. Can you believe that? A whole committee dedicated to mailbox colors. They meet on the first Tuesday of every month at the community center. I went to a community center once for a CPR certification class for work. The dummy they use for CPR is so creepy, with those blank eyes... \n\nUm... sorry. What did you ask me? About my father? Yeah. I just feel so guilty. Like I ruined everything and I deserve this punishment.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way about being punished and having a hollow feeling inside?\n\nPatient: It's been... gosh, about a year and two months now. It all started when my father's health really took a turn and his behavior started changing, and I suddenly had to step in and do all the caregiving. \n\nCaregiving is just... it's a full-time job on top of a full-time job. And you have to deal with all the doctors' offices. The receptionist at his cardiologist's office, Brenda, she is always chewing this incredibly loud peppermint gum. Snap, snap, snap, the whole time you're trying to sign in. I don't know how the nurses stand it. One of the nurses there, a younger guy with glasses, he told me he buys his scrubs from this specific online retailer because they have extra deep pockets. He said he likes to keep a little notepad in there to write down patient preferences, which I thought was nice. My nephew has deep pockets in his cargo shorts, but he just fills them with rocks he finds at the park. He's five, and he's obsessed with geology. My sister bought him one of those rock tumbler kits for his birthday, but it makes this horrible grinding noise in their garage. They had to put it on a timer so it wouldn't run at night and wake up the neighbors. Their neighbor has a basset hound that howls at everything anyway, though. Even the mailman...\n\nWait, I'm sorry. I lost my train of thought again. What were we talking about? The timeline? Right. Yeah, about a year and two months. Since I had to start taking care of my dad.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in activities that you used to enjoy before your father's health declined?\n\nPatient: Pleasure? No... no, not really. I used to love working in my garden, like I said, but now I just look at it and I don't feel anything. I used to read a lot, too. Mostly historical fiction. I had this whole collection of books about the Tudor dynasty. \n\nThe Tudors are just fascinating, aren't they? All that drama with Henry VIII and his wives. I went to a museum exhibit once that had some of the actual clothing from that era. The embroidery was unbelievable. They used real gold thread in some of the gowns. I tried to learn embroidery once, during a phase I went through in my thirties. I bought all these little wooden hoops and hundreds of different colored flosses. I even got a special carrying case for it all from a craft store that was going out of business. It was sad when that store closed, actually. The owner was this sweet older lady named Martha who always gave out hard candies at the register. Butterscotch, usually. I'm not a big fan of butterscotch, honestly. It always gets stuck in my teeth. My dentist told me I need to be careful with hard candies anyway because I have a filling on my back molar that's getting a little loose. He's a nice guy, my dentist. He has this aquarium in the waiting room with these tiny neon fish...\n\nI'm sorry, I did it again, didn't I? I just... my mind just wanders. But no, I don't really enjoy anything anymore. It all just feels like too much effort for nothing.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you lost a significant amount of weight since you started feeling this way?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. If the question touches several topics, return the one it is\n  most directly about — never more than one.\n- Copy `key`, `name` and `description` exactly as they appear in [Symptom Profiles].\n- If your profile does not cover what the doctor asked, return the text \"None\" for `key`,\n  `name` and `description`, and say why in `reason`. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Weight? Um... I don't think so. I mean, I haven't really been paying attention to the scale or anything. I guess my clothes fit about the same. 

I buy most of my clothes from this catalog that comes in the mail. They always have these really nice, sensible slacks for work. Work has been so strange lately, though. We just hired a new payroll specialist, and she insists on bringing her own ergonomic chair to the office every day. She literally rolls it from the parking lot into the elevator. It has this weird mesh back and all these levers. I asked her about it once in the breakroom, and she gave me a twenty-minute lecture on lumbar support. The breakroom microwave has been broken for a week, by the way. Someone tried to heat up a piece of fish wrapped in aluminum foil, and it sparked everywhere. The smell was awful. It reminded me of this seafood restaurant my parents used to take me to when I was a kid. They had these giant tanks with lobsters in them right by the front door. I always felt bad for the lobsters, with those rubber bands on their claws. Rubber bands are so useful, though. I have a whole ball of them in my desk drawer at home. I started collecting them off the morning newspaper. The delivery guy always throws it right into the rosebushes instead of on the porch...

Oh, sorry. I'm rambling again. But no, I haven't lost any weight that I've noticed.

Harming myself? I mean... I don't have a plan or anything like that. But I do catch myself thinking it would just be easier if I didn't wake up. Especially on mornings when I know there are going to be more calls from the care agency, and just... problems waiting at work. 

Work is just a lot right now. HR is supposed to be about helping people, but mostly it's just dealing with complaints. Like yesterday, someone complained about the new soap in the bathrooms. They said it smells too much like lavender and it's giving them a headache. Lavender is supposed to be calming, though, isn't it? I bought a lavender essential oil diffuser once to try and help me sleep. It was this little ceramic thing shaped like an owl. I put it on my nightstand, but the little light on it was too bright, and it made this weird bubbling sound. It sounded exactly like my neighbor's fish filter. He has this massive koi pond in his backyard. He spent all summer digging it out by hand. He said it was good exercise, but he ended up pulling a muscle in his back and had to go to physical therapy for a month. Physical therapy is so expensive, even with insurance. The co-pays really add up. My dad's co-pays for his specialists are just astronomical. And they always want you to pay right at the desk before you even see the doctor. Brenda, that receptionist I mentioned with the gum, she's a real stickler about it. She won't even let you sit down until you've handed over your credit card...

Wait, what was the question? Oh, right. Harming myself. No, no plan. Just... sometimes wishing I wouldn't wake up.

They meet on the first Tuesday of every month at the community center. I went to a community center once for a CPR certification class for work. The dummy they use for CPR is so creepy, with those blank eyes... \n\nUm... sorry. What did you ask me? About my father? Yeah. I just feel so guilty. Like I ruined everything and I deserve this punishment.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way about being punished and having a hollow feeling inside?\n\nPatient: It's been... gosh, about a year and two months now. Snap, snap, snap, the whole time you're trying to sign in. I don't know how the nurses stand it. One of the nurses there, a younger guy with glasses, he told me he buys his scrubs from this specific online retailer because they have extra deep pockets. He said he likes to keep a little notepad in there to write down patient preferences, which I thought was nice. My nephew has deep pockets in his cargo shorts, but he just fills them with rocks he finds at the park. He's five, and he's obsessed with geology. My sister bought him one of those rock tumbler kits for his birthday, but it makes this horrible grinding noise in their garage. They had to put it on a timer so it wouldn't run at night and wake up the neighbors. Their neighbor has a basset hound that howls at everything anyway, though. Even the mailman...\n\nWait, I'm sorry. I lost my train of thought again. What were we talking about? The timeline? Right. Yeah, about a year and two months. Since I had to start taking care of my dad.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in activities that you used to enjoy before your father's health declined?\n\nPatient: Pleasure? No... no, not really. I used to love working in my garden, like I said, but now I just look at it and I don't feel anything. I used to read a lot, too. Mostly historical fiction. I had this whole collection of books about the Tudor dynasty. \n\nThe Tudors are just fascinating, aren't they? All that drama with Henry VIII and his wives. I went to a museum exhibit once that had some of the actual clothing from that era. The embroidery was unbelievable. They used real gold thread in some of the gowns. I tried to learn embroidery once, during a phase I went through in my thirties. I bought all these little wooden hoops and hundreds of different colored flosses. I even got a special carrying case for it all from a craft store that was going out of business. It was sad when that store closed, actually. The owner was this sweet older lady named Martha who always gave out hard candies at the register. Butterscotch, usually. I'm not a big fan of butterscotch, honestly. We just hired a new payroll specialist, and she insists on bringing her own ergonomic chair to the office every day. She literally rolls it from the parking lot into the elevator. It has this weird mesh back and all these levers. I asked her about it once in the breakroom, and she gave me a twenty-minute lecture on lumbar support. The breakroom microwave has been broken for a week, by the way. Someone tried to heat up a piece of fish wrapped in aluminum foil, and it sparked everywhere. The smell was awful. It reminded me of this seafood restaurant my parents used to take me to when I was a kid. They had these giant tanks with lobsters in them right by the front door. I always felt bad for the lobsters, with those rubber bands on their claws. Rubber bands are so useful, though. I have a whole ball of them in my desk drawer at home. I started collecting them off the morning newspaper. The delivery guy always throws it right into the rosebushes instead of on the porch...\n\nOh, sorry. I'm rambling again. But no, I haven't lost any weight that I've noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of harming yourself since your father's health declined?\n\nPatient: Harming myself? I mean... I don't have a plan or anything like that. But I do catch myself thinking it would just be easier if I didn't wake up. Especially on mornings when I know there are going to be more calls from the care agency, and just... problems waiting at work. \n\nWork is just a lot right now. I put it on my nightstand, but the little light on it was too bright, and it made this weird bubbling sound. It sounded exactly like my neighbor's fish filter. He has this massive koi pond in his backyard. He spent all summer digging it out by hand. He said it was good exercise, but he ended up pulling a muscle in his back and had to go to physical therapy for a month. Physical therapy is so expensive, even with insurance. The owner was this sweet older lady named Martha who always gave out hard candies at the register. Butterscotch, usually. I'm not a big fan of butterscotch, honestly. It always gets stuck in my teeth. My dentist told me I need to be careful with hard candies anyway because I have a filling on my back molar that's getting a little loose. He's a nice guy, my dentist. He has this aquarium in the waiting room with these tiny neon fish...\n\nI'm sorry, I did it again, didn't I? I just... my mind just wanders. But no, I don't really enjoy anything anymore. It all just feels like too much effort for nothing.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Weight? Um... I don't think so. I mean, I haven't really been paying attention to the scale or anything. I guess my clothes fit about the same. \n\nI buy most of my clothes from this catalog that comes in the mail. They always have these really nice, sensible slacks for work. Work has been so strange lately, though. We just hired a new payroll specialist, and she insists on bringing her own ergonomic chair to the office every day. She literally rolls it from the parking lot into the elevator. It has this weird mesh back and all these levers. I asked her about it once in the breakroom, and she gave me a twenty-minute lecture on lumbar support. The breakroom microwave has been broken for a week, by the way. Someone tried to heat up a piece of fish wrapped in aluminum foil, and it sparked everywhere. The smell was awful. It reminded me of this seafood restaurant my parents used to take me to when I was a kid. They had these giant tanks with lobsters in them right by the front door. I always felt bad for the lobsters, with those rubber bands on their claws. Rubber bands are so useful, though. I have a whole ball of them in my desk drawer at home. I started collecting them off the morning newspaper. The delivery guy always throws it right into the rosebushes instead of on the porch...\n\nOh, sorry. I'm rambling again. But no, I haven't lost any weight that I've noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of harming yourself since your father's health declined?\n\nPatient: Harming myself? I mean... I don't have a plan or anything like that. But I do catch myself thinking it would just be easier if I didn't wake up. Especially on mornings when I know there are going to be more calls from the care agency, and just... problems waiting at work. \n\nWork is just a lot right now. HR is supposed to be about helping people, but mostly it's just dealing with complaints. Like yesterday, someone complained about the new soap in the bathrooms. They said it smells too much like lavender and it's giving them a headache. Lavender is supposed to be calming, though, isn't it? I bought a lavender essential oil diffuser once to try and help me sleep. It was this little ceramic thing shaped like an owl. I put it on my nightstand, but the little light on it was too bright, and it made this weird bubbling sound. It sounded exactly like my neighbor's fish filter. He has this massive koi pond in his backyard. He spent all summer digging it out by hand. He said it was good exercise, but he ended up pulling a muscle in his back and had to go to physical therapy for a month. Physical therapy is so expensive, even with insurance. The co-pays really add up. My dad's co-pays for his specialists are just astronomical. And they always want you to pay right at the desk before you even see the doctor. Brenda, that receptionist I mentioned with the gum, she's a real stickler about it. She won't even let you sit down until you've handed over your credit card...\n\nWait, what was the question? Oh, right. Harming myself. No, no plan. Just... sometimes wishing I wouldn't wake up.\n\n[Last Question]\nAre there any activities that you still enjoy or find pleasure in?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 46 / human_resources_worker / bachelors\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Activities I still enjoy? I don't know... I really can't think of anything. Nothing sounds good anymore. Even the things I used to like, like watching those cooking shows on the weekends. 

I used to watch this one where they have to bake an entire cake that looks like an everyday object, like a shoe or a handbag. It's crazy what people can do with fondant. Fondant tastes terrible, though. It's basically just sugary Play-Doh. I made a cake for my niece's birthday a few years ago and tried to use it, and it was a disaster. It kept tearing, and it got all sticky. My niece is seven now. She just started taking gymnastics classes. My sister says she's really good at the balance beam, but she keeps getting these terrible blisters on her hands from the uneven bars. They make this special chalk for gymnasts to use, I guess. It comes in these big white blocks. It reminds me of the chalk we used to have in elementary school, before whiteboards were a thing. Our math teacher, Mr. Henderson, he used to throw the chalk eraser at the board when he got frustrated. It would leave this big cloud of dust everywhere. He was always wearing these wool sweaters, even in the spring, and they would just get covered in white dust... 

I'm sorry, I lost track again. But no, to answer your question, there isn't really anything I find pleasure in right now. It's all just kind of blank.

Getting out of bed... yeah, it takes a lot of effort just to get my feet on the floor. And once I'm up, everything is just dragging. People at work have actually started asking if I'm sick because I move so slowly through all my routine tasks now. And in meetings, it's like... someone will ask me a really simple question, and it takes me a noticeable moment to even process it and answer them.

I tried looking up window treatments online to help the office manager, but there are just so many options. Venetian, roller, cellular shades... Cellular shades look like little honeycombs. My grandfather used to keep bees when I was little. He had these big white suits with the mesh veils, and he'd give us fresh honeycomb to chew on. It was so sweet, but the wax would always get stuck in your teeth. I hate getting things stuck in my teeth. I always keep a pack of those little plastic flossers in my purse now. We just hired a new payroll specialist, and she insists on bringing her own ergonomic chair to the office every day. She literally rolls it from the parking lot into the elevator. The breakroom microwave has been broken for a week, by the way. Someone tried to heat up a piece of fish wrapped in aluminum foil, and it sparked everywhere. The smell was awful. It reminded me of this seafood restaurant my parents used to take me to when I was a kid. They had these giant tanks with lobsters in them right by the front door. I always felt bad for the lobsters, with those rubber bands on their claws. Rubber bands are so useful, though. I have a whole ball of them in my desk drawer at home. I started collecting them off the morning newspaper. The delivery guy always throws it right into the rosebushes instead of on the porch...\n\nOh, sorry. I'm rambling again. But no, I haven't lost any weight that I've noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of harming yourself since your father's health declined?\n\nPatient: Harming myself? I mean... I don't have a plan or anything like that. But I do catch myself thinking it would just be easier if I didn't wake up. Especially on mornings when I know there are going to be more calls from the care agency, and just... problems waiting at work. \n\nWork is just a lot right now. HR is supposed to be about helping people, but mostly it's just dealing with complaints. Like yesterday, someone complained about the new soap in the bathrooms. They said it smells too much like lavender and it's giving them a headache. Lavender is supposed to be calming, though, isn't it? I bought a lavender essential oil diffuser once to try and help me sleep. It was this little ceramic thing shaped like an owl. I put it on my nightstand, but the little light on it was too bright, and it made this weird bubbling sound. It sounded exactly like my neighbor's fish filter. He has this massive koi pond in his backyard. He spent all summer digging it out by hand. He said it was good exercise, but he ended up pulling a muscle in his back and had to go to physical therapy for a month. Physical therapy is so expensive, even with insurance. It's all just kind of blank.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it harder to get out of bed in the morning and start your daily activities than you used to?\n\nPatient: Getting out of bed... yeah, it takes a lot of effort just to get my feet on the floor. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Avoiding social things? Yeah, I guess so. But it's more than just not going out. It's like... I just can't keep it together for anything. I've been missing deadlines at work, and I've completely started avoiding those difficult employee mediation meetings I normally handle. I used to be really good at those. And I even stopped showing up for my volunteering at the library because I just can't keep myself steady enough to follow through with it. 

I used to love the library. I helped shelve the returned books on Saturday mornings. It's so quiet there before they open. They have this one section for large print books, mostly romance novels and mysteries. The covers on those romance novels are always so dramatic, you know? Always some guy with no shirt on riding a horse or standing on a cliff. My aunt used to read those all the time. She had a whole bookshelf dedicated to them in her guest room. Whenever we visited, my sister and I would sneak in there and read the backs of them and giggle. My sister has this really loud, snorting laugh. She got it from our mom. My mom would always snort when she laughed really hard, especially when she was watching those old British sitcoms on PBS. They always had those laugh tracks that sounded a bit tinny... 

Wait, sorry. What were we talking about? Oh, right, avoiding things. Yeah, I just can't manage the volunteering or the meetings anymore. Everything is just falling apart.

My nephew has deep pockets in his cargo shorts, but he just fills them with rocks he finds at the park. He's five, and he's obsessed with geology. My sister bought him one of those rock tumbler kits for his birthday, but it makes this horrible grinding noise in their garage. They had to put it on a timer so it wouldn't run at night and wake up the neighbors. Their neighbor has a basset hound that howls at everything anyway, though. Even the mailman...\n\nWait, I'm sorry. I lost my train of thought again. What were we talking about? The timeline? Right. Yeah, about a year and two months. Since I had to start taking care of my dad.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in activities that you used to enjoy before your father's health declined?\n\nPatient: Pleasure? No... no, not really. I used to love working in my garden, like I said, but now I just look at it and I don't feel anything. I used to read a lot, too. Mostly historical fiction. I had this whole collection of books about the Tudor dynasty. \n\nThe Tudors are just fascinating, aren't they? All that drama with Henry VIII and his wives. I went to a museum exhibit once that had some of the actual clothing from that era. The embroidery was unbelievable. They used real gold thread in some of the gowns. I tried to learn embroidery once, during a phase I went through in my thirties. I bought all these little wooden hoops and hundreds of different colored flosses. I even got a special carrying case for it all from a craft store that was going out of business. It was sad when that store closed, actually. The owner was this sweet older lady named Martha who always gave out hard candies at the register. Butterscotch, usually. I'm not a big fan of butterscotch, honestly. It always gets stuck in my teeth. My dentist told me I need to be careful with hard candies anyway because I have a filling on my back molar that's getting a little loose. He's a nice guy, my dentist. He has this aquarium in the waiting room with these tiny neon fish...\n\nI'm sorry, I did it again, didn't I? I just... my mind just wanders. But no, I don't really enjoy anything anymore. It all just feels like too much effort for nothing.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Weight? Um... I don't think so. I mean, I haven't really been paying attention to the scale or anything. I guess my clothes fit about the same. \n\nI buy most of my clothes from this catalog that comes in the mail. They always have these really nice, sensible slacks for work. Work has been so strange lately, though. We just hired a new payroll specialist, and she insists on bringing her own ergonomic chair to the office every day. She literally rolls it from the parking lot into the elevator. It has this weird mesh back and all these levers. I asked her about it once in the breakroom, and she gave me a twenty-minute lecture on lumbar support. The breakroom microwave has been broken for a week, by the way. Someone tried to heat up a piece of fish wrapped in aluminum foil, and it sparked everywhere. The smell was awful. It reminded me of this seafood restaurant my parents used to take me to when I was a kid. They had these giant tanks with lobsters in them right by the front door. I always felt bad for the lobsters, with those rubber bands on their claws. Rubber bands are so useful, though. I have a whole ball of them in my desk drawer at home. I started collecting them off the morning newspaper. The delivery guy always throws it right into the rosebushes instead of on the porch...\n\nOh, sorry. I'm rambling again. But no, I haven't lost any weight that I've noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of harming yourself since your father's health declined?\n\nPatient: Harming myself? I mean... I don't have a plan or anything like that. But I do catch myself thinking it would just be easier if I didn't wake up. Especially on mornings when I know there are going to be more calls from the care agency, and just... problems waiting at work. \n\nWork is just a lot right now. HR is supposed to be about helping people, but mostly it's just dealing with complaints. Like yesterday, someone complained about the new soap in the bathrooms. They said it smells too much like lavender and it's giving them a headache. Lavender is supposed to be calming, though, isn't it? I bought a lavender essential oil diffuser once to try and help me sleep. It was this little ceramic thing shaped like an owl. I put it on my nightstand, but the little light on it was too bright, and it made this weird bubbling sound. It sounded exactly like my neighbor's fish filter. He has this massive koi pond in his backyard. He spent all summer digging it out by hand. He said it was good exercise, but he ended up pulling a muscle in his back and had to go to physical therapy for a month. Physical therapy is so expensive, even with insurance. The co-pays really add up. My dad's co-pays for his specialists are just astronomical. And they always want you to pay right at the desk before you even see the doctor. Brenda, that receptionist I mentioned with the gum, she's a real stickler about it. She won't even let you sit down until you've handed over your credit card...\n\nWait, what was the question? Oh, right. Harming myself. No, no plan. Just... sometimes wishing I wouldn't wake up.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy or find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Activities I still enjoy? I don't know... I really can't think of anything. Nothing sounds good anymore. Even the things I used to like, like watching those cooking shows on the weekends. \n\nI used to watch this one where they have to bake an entire cake that looks like an everyday object, like a shoe or a handbag. It's crazy what people can do with fondant. Fondant tastes terrible, though. It's basically just sugary Play-Doh. I made a cake for my niece's birthday a few years ago and tried to use it, and it was a disaster. It kept tearing, and it got all sticky. My niece is seven now. She just started taking gymnastics classes. My sister says she's really good at the balance beam, but she keeps getting these terrible blisters on her hands from the uneven bars. They make this special chalk for gymnasts to use, I guess. It comes in these big white blocks. It reminds me of the chalk we used to have in elementary school, before whiteboards were a thing. Our math teacher, Mr. Henderson, he used to throw the chalk eraser at the board when he got frustrated. It would leave this big cloud of dust everywhere. He was always wearing these wool sweaters, even in the spring, and they would just get covered in white dust... \n\nI'm sorry, I lost track again. But no, to answer your question, there isn't really anything I find pleasure in right now. It's all just kind of blank.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it harder to get out of bed in the morning and start your daily activities than you used to?\n\nPatient: Getting out of bed... yeah, it takes a lot of effort just to get my feet on the floor. And in meetings, it's like... someone will ask me a really simple question, and it takes me a noticeable moment to even process it and answer them. \n\nWe have most of our HR meetings in the big glass conference room on the second floor. The sun glares right through those windows in the afternoon. They really need to install some blinds. I tried looking up window treatments online to help the office manager, but there are just so many options. Venetian, roller, cellular shades... Cellular shades look like little honeycombs. My grandfather used to keep bees when I was little. He had these big white suits with the mesh veils, and he'd give us fresh honeycomb to chew on. It was so sweet, but the wax would always get stuck in your teeth. I hate getting things stuck in my teeth. I always keep a pack of those little plastic flossers in my purse now. The mint flavored ones. Though honestly, the mint flavor doesn't last very long. It's kind of like that gum Brenda chews, the receptionist I was telling you about... she's always popping it while she waits for people's credit cards... \n\nI'm sorry, I forgot what you asked me. Small things? I don't know... I really don't think so. I can't think of anything that makes me feel happy right now. It's all just kind of empty. Even the little things that used to be nice, like getting a coffee on the way to work. 

I used to stop at this little drive-thru place, the one with the green awning near the highway on-ramp. They always had these little dog treats they'd give out if you had your dog in the car. I don't have a dog, but the woman in front of me always did. A golden retriever, I think. It would stick its head all the way out the window. Golden retrievers shed so much, though. My neighbor has one, and I swear there are tumbleweeds of dog hair blowing across my driveway every time the wind picks up. It's really frustrating when I'm trying to sweep the garage out. I bought this heavy-duty push broom from the hardware store last month to try and keep up with it. The bristles are supposed to be made from recycled plastic bottles, which I guess is good for the environment. I try to recycle when I can, but our city's recycling program is so confusing. They changed the rules last year, and now you can't put glass in the blue bins anymore. You have to take it to a separate drop-off center. It's such a hassle. The drop-off center is all the way across town, right next to that old bowling alley that closed down...

I'm sorry, what did you ask me? About being happy? Yeah, no, I don't really feel any of that anymore. Not even a little bit.

And they always just tell her to submit another ticket. I don't know how she has the patience for it, honestly. If it were me, I would have just walked down to their office on the second floor and demanded someone look at my computer. They keep the AC so high down there, though, it's freezing. I always have to bring a cardigan if I go to that floor... um... I'm sorry, what did you ask me? About the punishment? Right. No, I don't do anything to try and alleviate it. There's nothing I can do to fix it anyway.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or empty most of the time since you started feeling like you're being punished?\n\nPatient: Yeah... I guess sad is one word for it. It's more like... most days I wake up with this heavy, hollow feeling. Like the color has just gone out of everything. I can sit at my desk in HR, or stare out at my garden beds at home, and it just feels like none of it reaches me anymore. It's all just blank. \n\nSpeaking of the garden, my hydrangeas are completely dying. I tried putting coffee grounds in the soil—my neighbor, Mrs. Gable, swore by it. She has these massive, bright blue blooms every summer. She told me the acidity is the trick, but honestly, I think she just has better soil. Her husband used to be a landscaper before he retired. They have this gorgeous stone retaining wall he built himself. It took him two summers to finish it because he insisted on sourcing the stones from this specific quarry upstate. I remember they had a flat tire on the way back from the quarry once, and they had to wait four hours for a tow truck because it was a holiday weekend. Oh, I hate driving on holiday weekends. The traffic on the interstate is just a nightmare. Last Labor Day, I was stuck behind a semi-truck for forty miles... \n\nWait, what was I saying? Oh, right. The hollow feeling. Yeah, it's just... it's there all the time. Like I'm just empty inside.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty about what happened with your father?\n\nPatient: Guilty? Yes. I... I keep going over every single decision I made about his appointments. Every time I had to reschedule because of work, every tense conversation with my relatives about who was going to check on him. I just end up feeling like I'm this incredibly selfish daughter. And a useless employee, too, honestly. I feel like I just deserve whatever happens to me now. \n\nIt's funny how those conversations with relatives go, isn't it? My Aunt Susan, she always had an opinion about everything, but she never actually offered to drive him. She lives in this massive house out in the suburbs, the kind with the wrap-around porch. She had it painted this pale yellow color last spring. The painters left a mess on her driveway, though—paint chips everywhere. She spent three weeks fighting with the contractor about getting it power-washed. She's always fighting with someone. Last year it was the homeowner's association because they said her mailbox was the wrong shade of white. Can you believe that? A whole committee dedicated to mailbox colors. They meet on the first Tuesday of every month at the community center. I went to a community center once for a CPR certification class for work. The dummy they use for CPR is so creepy, with those blank eyes... \n\nUm... sorry. What did you ask me? About my father? Yeah. I just feel so guilty. Like I ruined everything and I deserve this punishment.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way about being punished and having a hollow feeling inside?\n\nPatient: It's been... gosh, about a year and two months now. It all started when my father's health really took a turn and his behavior started changing, and I suddenly had to step in and do all the caregiving. \n\nCaregiving is just... it's a full-time job on top of a full-time job. And you have to deal with all the doctors' offices. The receptionist at his cardiologist's office, Brenda, she is always chewing this incredibly loud peppermint gum. Snap, snap, snap, the whole time you're trying to sign in. I don't know how the nurses stand it. One of the nurses there, a younger guy with glasses, he told me he buys his scrubs from this specific online retailer because they have extra deep pockets. He said he likes to keep a little notepad in there to write down patient preferences, which I thought was nice. My nephew has deep pockets in his cargo shorts, but he just fills them with rocks he finds at the park. He's five, and he's obsessed with geology. My sister bought him one of those rock tumbler kits for his birthday, but it makes this horrible grinding noise in their garage. They had to put it on a timer so it wouldn't run at night and wake up the neighbors. Their neighbor has a basset hound that howls at everything anyway, though. Even the mailman...\n\nWait, I'm sorry. I lost my train of thought again. What were we talking about? The timeline? Right. Yeah, about a year and two months. Since I had to start taking care of my dad.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in activities that you used to enjoy before your father's health declined?\n\nPatient: Pleasure? No... no, not really. I used to love working in my garden, like I said, but now I just look at it and I don't feel anything. I used to read a lot, too. Mostly historical fiction. I had this whole collection of books about the Tudor dynasty. \n\nThe Tudors are just fascinating, aren't they? All that drama with Henry VIII and his wives. I went to a museum exhibit once that had some of the actual clothing from that era. The embroidery was unbelievable. They used real gold thread in some of the gowns. I tried to learn embroidery once, during a phase I went through in my thirties. I bought all these little wooden hoops and hundreds of different colored flosses. I even got a special carrying case for it all from a craft store that was going out of business. It was sad when that store closed, actually. The owner was this sweet older lady named Martha who always gave out hard candies at the register. Butterscotch, usually. I'm not a big fan of butterscotch, honestly. It always gets stuck in my teeth. My dentist told me I need to be careful with hard candies anyway because I have a filling on my back molar that's getting a little loose. He's a nice guy, my dentist. He has this aquarium in the waiting room with these tiny neon fish...\n\nI'm sorry, I did it again, didn't I? I just... my mind just wanders. But no, I don't really enjoy anything anymore. It all just feels like too much effort for nothing.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Weight? Um... I don't think so. I mean, I haven't really been paying attention to the scale or anything. I guess my clothes fit about the same. \n\nI buy most of my clothes from this catalog that comes in the mail. They always have these really nice, sensible slacks for work. Work has been so strange lately, though. We just hired a new payroll specialist, and she insists on bringing her own ergonomic chair to the office every day. She literally rolls it from the parking lot into the elevator. It has this weird mesh back and all these levers. I asked her about it once in the breakroom, and she gave me a twenty-minute lecture on lumbar support. The breakroom microwave has been broken for a week, by the way. Someone tried to heat up a piece of fish wrapped in aluminum foil, and it sparked everywhere. The smell was awful. It reminded me of this seafood restaurant my parents used to take me to when I was a kid. They had these giant tanks with lobsters in them right by the front door. I always felt bad for the lobsters, with those rubber bands on their claws. Rubber bands are so useful, though. I have a whole ball of them in my desk drawer at home. I started collecting them off the morning newspaper. The delivery guy always throws it right into the rosebushes instead of on the porch...\n\nOh, sorry. I'm rambling again. But no, I haven't lost any weight that I've noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of harming yourself since your father's health declined?\n\nPatient: Harming myself? I mean... I don't have a plan or anything like that. But I do catch myself thinking it would just be easier if I didn't wake up. Especially on mornings when I know there are going to be more calls from the care agency, and just... problems waiting at work. \n\nWork is just a lot right now. HR is supposed to be about helping people, but mostly it's just dealing with complaints. Like yesterday, someone complained about the new soap in the bathrooms. They said it smells too much like lavender and it's giving them a headache. Lavender is supposed to be calming, though, isn't it? I bought a lavender essential oil diffuser once to try and help me sleep. It was this little ceramic thing shaped like an owl. I put it on my nightstand, but the little light on it was too bright, and it made this weird bubbling sound. It sounded exactly like my neighbor's fish filter. He has this massive koi pond in his backyard. He spent all summer digging it out by hand. He said it was good exercise, but he ended up pulling a muscle in his back and had to go to physical therapy for a month. Physical therapy is so expensive, even with insurance. The co-pays really add up. My dad's co-pays for his specialists are just astronomical. And they always want you to pay right at the desk before you even see the doctor. Brenda, that receptionist I mentioned with the gum, she's a real stickler about it. She won't even let you sit down until you've handed over your credit card...\n\nWait, what was the question? Oh, right. Harming myself. No, no plan. Just... sometimes wishing I wouldn't wake up.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy or find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Activities I still enjoy? I don't know... I really can't think of anything. Nothing sounds good anymore. Even the things I used to like, like watching those cooking shows on the weekends. \n\nI used to watch this one where they have to bake an entire cake that looks like an everyday object, like a shoe or a handbag. It's crazy what people can do with fondant. Fondant tastes terrible, though. It's basically just sugary Play-Doh. I made a cake for my niece's birthday a few years ago and tried to use it, and it was a disaster. It kept tearing, and it got all sticky. My niece is seven now. She just started taking gymnastics classes. My sister says she's really good at the balance beam, but she keeps getting these terrible blisters on her hands from the uneven bars. They make this special chalk for gymnasts to use, I guess. It comes in these big white blocks. It reminds me of the chalk we used to have in elementary school, before whiteboards were a thing. Our math teacher, Mr. Henderson, he used to throw the chalk eraser at the board when he got frustrated. It would leave this big cloud of dust everywhere. He was always wearing these wool sweaters, even in the spring, and they would just get covered in white dust... \n\nI'm sorry, I lost track again. But no, to answer your question, there isn't really anything I find pleasure in right now. It's all just kind of blank.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it harder to get out of bed in the morning and start your daily activities than you used to?\n\nPatient: Getting out of bed... yeah, it takes a lot of effort just to get my feet on the floor. And once I'm up, everything is just dragging. People at work have actually started asking if I'm sick because I move so slowly through all my routine tasks now. And in meetings, it's like... someone will ask me a really simple question, and it takes me a noticeable moment to even process it and answer them. \n\nWe have most of our HR meetings in the big glass conference room on the second floor. The sun glares right through those windows in the afternoon. They really need to install some blinds. I tried looking up window treatments online to help the office manager, but there are just so many options. Venetian, roller, cellular shades... Cellular shades look like little honeycombs. My grandfather used to keep bees when I was little. He had these big white suits with the mesh veils, and he'd give us fresh honeycomb to chew on. It was so sweet, but the wax would always get stuck in your teeth. I hate getting things stuck in my teeth. I always keep a pack of those little plastic flossers in my purse now. The mint flavored ones. Though honestly, the mint flavor doesn't last very long. It's kind of like that gum Brenda chews, the receptionist I was telling you about... she's always popping it while she waits for people's credit cards... \n\nI'm sorry, I forgot what you asked me. Was it about the mornings? Yeah, everything is just really slow right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding social interactions or events more than you used to?\n\nPatient: Avoiding social things? Yeah, I guess so. But it's more than just not going out. It's like... I just can't keep it together for anything. I've been missing deadlines at work, and I've completely started avoiding those difficult employee mediation meetings I normally handle. I used to be really good at those. And I even stopped showing up for my volunteering at the library because I just can't keep myself steady enough to follow through with it. \n\nI used to love the library. I helped shelve the returned books on Saturday mornings. It's so quiet there before they open. They have this one section for large print books, mostly romance novels and mysteries. The covers on those romance novels are always so dramatic, you know? Always some guy with no shirt on riding a horse or standing on a cliff. My aunt used to read those all the time. She had a whole bookshelf dedicated to them in her guest room. Whenever we visited, my sister and I would sneak in there and read the backs of them and giggle. My sister has this really loud, snorting laugh. She got it from our mom. My mom would always snort when she laughed really hard, especially when she was watching those old British sitcoms on PBS. They always had those laugh tracks that sounded a bit tinny... \n\nWait, sorry. What were we talking about? Oh, right, avoiding things. Yeah, I just can't manage the volunteering or the meetings anymore. Everything is just falling apart.\n\nDoctor: Are there any small things that still bring you a little bit of joy or happiness?\n\nPatient: Small things? I don't know... I really don't think so. I can't think of anything that makes me feel happy right now. It's all just kind of empty. Even the little things that used to be nice, like getting a coffee on the way to work. \n\nI used to stop at this little drive-thru place, the one with the green awning near the highway on-ramp. They always had these little dog treats they'd give out if you had your dog in the car. I don't have a dog, but the woman in front of me always did. A golden retriever, I think. It would stick its head all the way out the window. Golden retrievers shed so much, though. My neighbor has one, and I swear there are tumbleweeds of dog hair blowing across my driveway every time the wind picks up. It's really frustrating when I'm trying to sweep the garage out. I bought this heavy-duty push broom from the hardware store last month to try and keep up with it. The bristles are supposed to be made from recycled plastic bottles, which I guess is good for the environment. I try to recycle when I can, but our city's recycling program is so confusing. They changed the rules last year, and now you can't put glass in the blue bins anymore. You have to take it to a separate drop-off center. It's such a hassle. The drop-off center is all the way across town, right next to that old bowling alley that closed down...\n\nI'm sorry, what did you ask me? About being happy? Yeah, no, I don't really feel any of that anymore. Not even a little bit.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I always felt bad for the lobsters, with those rubber bands on their claws. Rubber bands are so useful, though. I have a whole ball of them in my desk drawer at home. My thoughts? Yeah, I guess... I mean, I don't really know if they're slowing down, exactly. It's hard to tell what's my body and what's my brain, you know? It's just all so heavy. 

Like when I try to read the emails at work, I just stare at the screen. We use this new email system now, they switched it over the weekend last month. It has this weird blue layout that hurts my eyes if I look at it too long. I bought these blue-light blocking glasses from the internet to help with it. They have these thick, black frames, kind of like the ones my high school biology teacher used to wear. Mr. Henderson. He was always talking about fruit flies. We had to do this whole experiment breeding them, and the classroom always smelled like rotting bananas because of the mash we had to feed them. Bananas go bad so fast on the counter. I tried freezing them once to make smoothies, but my blender is so old it just kind of chewed them up into these frozen chunks. I got that blender as a housewarming gift when I moved into my first apartment. It was a tiny little place, right above a bakery. The smell of the bread baking at 4 AM was amazing, but the delivery trucks were so loud...

Sorry. I don't know why I keep doing that. Um, my thoughts. I just... I don't know if they're slower. I'm just not sure. Everything is just hard.

Better off dead? Um... I don't know if I'd say it exactly like that. I don't have a plan to hurt myself or anything. But I do catch myself thinking sometimes that it would just be so much easier if I didn't wake up at all. Guilt about my dad? Um... yeah, I guess it's pretty specific. I keep going over every single decision I made about his appointments, and every tense conversation I've had with my relatives about his care. I just end up feeling like I'm this incredibly selfish daughter, and honestly, a useless employee too, like I just deserve whatever bad things happen to me. 

Speaking of my relatives, my cousin Sarah called me the other day. She lives out in Oregon now, near Portland, I think. She moved there a few years ago because her husband got a job at this tech company. They bought this beautiful house with a huge wrap-around porch. I always wanted a porch like that, where you could sit out in the morning with a cup of coffee and just watch the neighborhood. My neighbor across the street has a porch, but he never uses it. He just leaves his old newspapers piled up on the steps. The paperboy just keeps throwing them right on top of the pile every morning. It's one of those kids who rides his bike and just tosses them without looking. When I was a kid, the paperboy actually walked up to the door and put it in the mailbox. People don't really do that anymore, do they? Everyone just reads the news on their phones now. I tried reading the news on my phone, but the screen is just so small, it hurts my eyes if I stare at it too long. I had to get reading glasses last year, these little tortoiseshell ones...

Wait, sorry. I lost my train of thought. But yeah, I just feel so guilty about my dad, and like I'm failing at everything.

No... no, not really. I used to love working in my garden, like I said, but now I just look at it and I don't feel anything. I used to read a lot, too. Mostly historical fiction. I went to a museum exhibit once that had some of the actual clothing from that era. The embroidery was unbelievable. They used real gold thread in some of the gowns. But no, I don't really enjoy anything anymore. It all just feels like too much effort for nothing.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Weight? Um... I don't think so. The breakroom microwave has been broken for a week, by the way. Someone tried to heat up a piece of fish wrapped in aluminum foil, and it sparked everywhere. The smell was awful. It reminded me of this seafood restaurant my parents used to take me to when I was a kid. They had these giant tanks with lobsters in them right by the front door. I always felt bad for the lobsters, with those rubber bands on their claws. Rubber bands are so useful, though. I have a whole ball of them in my desk drawer at home. I started collecting them off the morning newspaper. The delivery guy always throws it right into the rosebushes instead of on the porch...\n\nOh, sorry. I'm rambling again. But no, I haven't lost any weight that I've noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of harming yourself since your father's health declined?\n\nPatient: Harming myself? I mean... They said it smells too much like lavender and it's giving them a headache. Lavender is supposed to be calming, though, isn't it? I bought a lavender essential oil diffuser once to try and help me sleep. It was this little ceramic thing shaped like an owl. I put it on my nightstand, but the little light on it was too bright, and it made this weird bubbling sound. It sounded exactly like my neighbor's fish filter. Specific things? Um... well, I just... I constantly replay everything in my head. Like, I keep going over every single decision I made about his doctors' appointments, trying to figure out if I picked the wrong specialist or scheduled things too late. And then I think about every tense conversation I've had with my relatives about his care, how I snapped at them or didn't explain things right. 

My aunt called last week, actually, the one who lives in Florida. She moved down there after she retired from the post office. She used to work at the main branch downtown, the one with the really heavy brass doors. They were always so hard to push open, especially in the winter when you had thick gloves on. I had this pair of leather gloves, they were lined with cashmere, and I lost one of them on the subway once. I was so upset because they were a gift from my old boss. He was a really nice guy, he always brought in donuts on Fridays from this little bakery near his house. They had the best crullers, you know, the twisted ones with the glaze? I tried making them at home once, but the dough just wouldn't fry right. It got all greasy and heavy. I think my oil wasn't hot enough. I need to get one of those candy thermometers that clips onto the side of the pot...

Oh, I'm sorry. I'm doing it again, aren't I? Um... but yeah, I just... I go over all those decisions and arguments, and I just end up feeling like I'm this incredibly selfish daughter. And honestly, it spills over into work too. I feel like a completely useless employee. I just feel like I deserve whatever bad things happen to me.

I guess sad is one word for it. It's more like... most days I wake up with this heavy, hollow feeling. Like the color has just gone out of everything. They have this gorgeous stone retaining wall he built himself. It took him two summers to finish it because he insisted on sourcing the stones from this specific quarry upstate. The traffic on the interstate is just a nightmare. Last Labor Day, I was stuck behind a semi-truck for forty miles... \n\nWait, what was I saying? Oh, right. The hollow feeling. Yeah, it's just... it's there all the time. Like I'm just empty inside.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty about what happened with your father?\n\nPatient: Guilty? Yes. I... I keep going over every single decision I made about his appointments. Every time I had to reschedule because of work, every tense conversation with my relatives about who was going to check on him. I just end up feeling like I'm this incredibly selfish daughter. And a useless employee, too, honestly. I feel like I just deserve whatever happens to me now. \n\nIt's funny how those conversations with relatives go, isn't it? My Aunt Susan, she always had an opinion about everything, but she never actually offered to drive him. She lives in this massive house out in the suburbs, the kind with the wrap-around porch. She had it painted this pale yellow color last spring. The painters left a mess on her driveway, though—paint chips everywhere. She spent three weeks fighting with the contractor about getting it power-washed. She's always fighting with someone. Last year it was the homeowner's association because they said her mailbox was the wrong shade of white. Can you believe that? A whole committee dedicated to mailbox colors. They meet on the first Tuesday of every month at the community center. I went to a community center once for a CPR certification class for work. He said he likes to keep a little notepad in there to write down patient preferences, which I thought was nice. My nephew has deep pockets in his cargo shorts, but he just fills them with rocks he finds at the park. He's five, and he's obsessed with geology. My sister bought him one of those rock tumbler kits for his birthday, but it makes this horrible grinding noise in their garage. They had to put it on a timer so it wouldn't run at night and wake up the neighbors. Their neighbor has a basset hound that howls at everything anyway, though. Even the mailman...\n\nWait, I'm sorry. I lost my train of thought again. What were we talking about? The timeline? Right. Yeah, about a year and two months. Since I had to start taking care of my dad.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in activities that you used to enjoy before your father's health declined?\n\nPatient: Pleasure? No... no, not really. I used to love working in my garden, like I said, but now I just look at it and I don't feel anything. I used to read a lot, too. Mostly historical fiction.
