But no, I haven't lost any weight that I've noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of harming yourself since your father's health declined?\n\nPatient: Harming myself? I mean... I don't have a plan or anything like that. But I do catch myself thinking it would just be easier if I didn't wake up. Especially on mornings when I know there are going to be more calls from the care agency, and just... problems waiting at work. \n\nWork is just a lot right now. HR is supposed to be about helping people, but mostly it's just dealing with complaints. Like yesterday, someone complained about the new soap in the bathrooms. They said it smells too much like lavender and it's giving them a headache. Lavender is supposed to be calming, though, isn't it? I bought a lavender essential oil diffuser once to try and help me sleep. It was this little ceramic thing shaped like an owl. I put it on my nightstand, but the little light on it was too bright, and it made this weird bubbling sound. It sounded exactly like my neighbor's fish filter. He has this massive koi pond in his backyard. He spent all summer digging it out by hand. He said it was good exercise, but he ended up pulling a muscle in his back and had to go to physical therapy for a month. Physical therapy is so expensive, even with insurance. The co-pays really add up. My dad's co-pays for his specialists are just astronomical. And they always want you to pay right at the desk before you even see the doctor. Brenda, that receptionist I mentioned with the gum, she's a real stickler about it. She won't even let you sit down until you've handed over your credit card...\n\nWait, what was the question? Oh, right. Harming myself. No, no plan. Just... sometimes wishing I wouldn't wake up.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy or find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Activities I still enjoy? I don't know... I really can't think of anything. Nothing sounds good anymore. Even the things I used to like, like watching those cooking shows on the weekends. \n\nI used to watch this one where they have to bake an entire cake that looks like an everyday object, like a shoe or a handbag. It's crazy what people can do with fondant. Fondant tastes terrible, though. It's basically just sugary Play-Doh. I made a cake for my niece's birthday a few years ago and tried to use it, and it was a disaster. It kept tearing, and it got all sticky. My niece is seven now. She just started taking gymnastics classes. My sister says she's really good at the balance beam, but she keeps getting these terrible blisters on her hands from the uneven bars. They make this special chalk for gymnasts to use, I guess. It comes in these big white blocks. It reminds me of the chalk we used to have in elementary school, before whiteboards were a thing. Our math teacher, Mr. Henderson, he used to throw the chalk eraser at the board when he got frustrated. It would leave this big cloud of dust everywhere. He was always wearing these wool sweaters, even in the spring, and they would just get covered in white dust... \n\nI'm sorry, I lost track again. But no, to answer your question, there isn't really anything I find pleasure in right now. It's all just kind of blank.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it harder to get out of bed in the morning and start your daily activities than you used to?\n\nPatient: Getting out of bed... yeah, it takes a lot of effort just to get my feet on the floor. And once I'm up, everything is just dragging. People at work have actually started asking if I'm sick because I move so slowly through all my routine tasks now. And in meetings, it's like... someone will ask me a really simple question, and it takes me a noticeable moment to even process it and answer them. \n\nWe have most of our HR meetings in the big glass conference room on the second floor. The sun glares right through those windows in the afternoon. They really need to install some blinds. I tried looking up window treatments online to help the office manager, but there are just so many options. Venetian, roller, cellular shades... Cellular shades look like little honeycombs. My grandfather used to keep bees when I was little. He had these big white suits with the mesh veils, and he'd give us fresh honeycomb to chew on. It was so sweet, but the wax would always get stuck in your teeth. I hate getting things stuck in my teeth. I always keep a pack of those little plastic flossers in my purse now. The mint flavored ones. Though honestly, the mint flavor doesn't last very long. It's kind of like that gum Brenda chews, the receptionist I was telling you about... she's always popping it while she waits for people's credit cards... \n\nI'm sorry, I forgot what you asked me. Was it about the mornings? Yeah, everything is just really slow right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding social interactions or events more than you used to?\n\nPatient: Avoiding social things? Yeah, I guess so. But it's more than just not going out. It's like... I just can't keep it together for anything. I've been missing deadlines at work, and I've completely started avoiding those difficult employee mediation meetings I normally handle. I used to be really good at those. And I even stopped showing up for my volunteering at the library because I just can't keep myself steady enough to follow through with it. \n\nI used to love the library. I helped shelve the returned books on Saturday mornings. It's so quiet there before they open. They have this one section for large print books, mostly romance novels and mysteries. The covers on those romance novels are always so dramatic, you know? Always some guy with no shirt on riding a horse or standing on a cliff. My aunt used to read those all the time. She had a whole bookshelf dedicated to them in her guest room. Whenever we visited, my sister and I would sneak in there and read the backs of them and giggle. My sister has this really loud, snorting laugh. She got it from our mom. My mom would always snort when she laughed really hard, especially when she was watching those old British sitcoms on PBS. They always had those laugh tracks that sounded a bit tinny... \n\nWait, sorry. What were we talking about? Oh, right, avoiding things. Yeah, I just can't manage the volunteering or the meetings anymore. Everything is just falling apart.\n\nDoctor: Are there any small things that still bring you a little bit of joy or happiness?\n\nPatient: Small things? I don't know... I really don't think so. I can't think of anything that makes me feel happy right now. It's all just kind of empty. Even the little things that used to be nice, like getting a coffee on the way to work. \n\nI used to stop at this little drive-thru place, the one with the green awning near the highway on-ramp. They always had these little dog treats they'd give out if you had your dog in the car. I don't have a dog, but the woman in front of me always did. A golden retriever, I think. It would stick its head all the way out the window. Golden retrievers shed so much, though. My neighbor has one, and I swear there are tumbleweeds of dog hair blowing across my driveway every time the wind picks up. It's really frustrating when I'm trying to sweep the garage out. I bought this heavy-duty push broom from the hardware store last month to try and keep up with it. The bristles are supposed to be made from recycled plastic bottles, which I guess is good for the environment. I try to recycle when I can, but our city's recycling program is so confusing. They changed the rules last year, and now you can't put glass in the blue bins anymore. You have to take it to a separate drop-off center. It's such a hassle. The drop-off center is all the way across town, right next to that old bowling alley that closed down...\n\nI'm sorry, what did you ask me? About being happy? Yeah, no, I don't really feel any of that anymore. Not even a little bit.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your movements or reactions have slowed down significantly since you've been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, I think I mentioned that a little bit ago. People at work have actually started asking if I'm sick because I'm just moving so slowly through all my normal stuff. And in meetings, if someone asks me a really simple question, it takes me a noticeable moment to even process it and answer. \n\nIt's really embarrassing, especially when Brenda is in the room. Brenda is the receptionist I was telling you about. She always has this brightly colored nail polish on. Last week it was neon pink. I don't know how she types with nails that long. I tried acrylics once for a friend's wedding, but I couldn't even unbutton my own coat. The wedding was outside in October, which is a terrible idea for this area because the weather is so unpredictable. It actually ended up sleeting during the vows. Everyone was shivering, and the bridesmaids' dresses were this thin chiffon material. Chiffon is so delicate, you have to dry clean it. Dry cleaning is getting so expensive lately. There's a place down the street from my office that charges eight dollars just for a blouse... \n\nWait, sorry. I'm doing it again, aren't I? What was the question? Oh, about moving slow. Yeah, everything just feels like I'm moving through molasses.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are slowing down as well, or is it just your physical movements?\n\nPatient: My thoughts? Yeah, I guess... I mean, I don't really know if they're slowing down, exactly. It's hard to tell what's my body and what's my brain, you know? It's just all so heavy. \n\nLike when I try to read the emails at work, I just stare at the screen. We use this new email system now, they switched it over the weekend last month. It has this weird blue layout that hurts my eyes if I look at it too long. I bought these blue-light blocking glasses from the internet to help with it. They have these thick, black frames, kind of like the ones my high school biology teacher used to wear. Mr. Henderson. He was always talking about fruit flies. We had to do this whole experiment breeding them, and the classroom always smelled like rotting bananas because of the mash we had to feed them. Bananas go bad so fast on the counter. I tried freezing them once to make smoothies, but my blender is so old it just kind of chewed them up into these frozen chunks. I got that blender as a housewarming gift when I moved into my first apartment. It was a tiny little place, right above a bakery. The smell of the bread baking at 4 AM was amazing, but the delivery trucks were so loud...\n\nSorry. I don't know why I keep doing that. Um, my thoughts. I just... I don't know if they're slower. I'm just not sure. Everything is just hard.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be better off dead or wish you could just disappear?\n\nPatient: Better off dead? Um... I don't know if I'd say it exactly like that. I don't have a plan to hurt myself or anything. But I do catch myself thinking sometimes that it would just be so much easier if I didn't wake up at all. Especially on those mornings when I know there are going to be more calls from the care agency about my mom, and all those problems waiting for me at work. \n\nMy mom's care agency is just so disorganized. They keep sending different people every week, and she hates that. Last Tuesday they sent this young girl, she couldn't have been older than twenty, and she was wearing these incredibly bright yellow sneakers. They were so bright they practically glowed in the dark. I used to have a pair of yellow sneakers when I was in college, I bought them for a charity run. It was a 5K for breast cancer awareness, I think. We had to run through this park downtown, the one with the big duck pond. The ducks there are so aggressive, though. If you even have a cracker in your hand, they'll swarm you. I dropped my keys near the edge of the pond once and a goose actually hissed at me when I tried to pick them up. Geese are terrifying. They have those teeth on their tongues... \n\nWait, what was I saying? Oh. Disappearing. Yeah, I just... I think about not waking up. It just seems like it would be a relief.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities, no matter how small, that you still look forward to or enjoy?\n\nPatient: Activities I enjoy? Um, I don't really know. Nothing, really. I used to like gardening, but I haven't even looked at my plants in weeks. They're probably all dead by now. I had this really beautiful peace lily in the living room. It was huge, I've had it for like five years. I bought it at this little nursery out in the suburbs, the one next to that Italian restaurant. Luigi's, I think it was called. They had the best garlic knots. They served them in these little wicker baskets with a red checkered cloth underneath. My grandmother used to have a tablecloth just like that in her kitchen. She always kept a jar of hard candies on the table, those strawberry ones with the wrappers that look like actual strawberries. The wrappers were so crinkly, they made such a loud noise when you opened them during church... \n\nSorry. I'm doing it again. Um, no. There's really nothing I look forward to anymore. It all just feels like a chore.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight unintentionally since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Weight? Um, no, I don't think so. I haven't really been paying attention to the scale, honestly. I don't eat much lately because nothing tastes good, but my clothes still fit about the same. I usually buy my clothes at that department store in the mall, the one with the big glass doors. They always have that perfume counter right at the front, and the ladies are always trying to spray you with samples. One time, this woman sprayed me with something that smelled exactly like my aunt's old potpourri. She used to keep bowls of it all over her house, these dried up rose petals and weird little pinecones. She collected pinecones whenever we went camping. We used to go to this state park up north, and the mosquitoes there were just awful. You had to spray yourself with bug spray constantly, the kind that leaves that sticky film on your skin. I hate that feeling, it makes me want to take a shower immediately... \n\nOh, wait. What were we talking about? Weight. Right. Yeah, no, I haven't really lost any weight that I've noticed.\n\nDoctor: Is your guilt about your father's decline focused on something specific you did or didn't do for him?\n\nPatient: Guilt about my dad? Um... yeah, I guess it's pretty specific. I keep going over every single decision I made about his appointments, and every tense conversation I've had with my relatives about his care. I just end up feeling like I'm this incredibly selfish daughter, and honestly, a useless employee too, like I just deserve whatever bad things happen to me. \n\nSpeaking of my relatives, my cousin Sarah called me the other day. She lives out in Oregon now, near Portland, I think. She moved there a few years ago because her husband got a job at this tech company. They bought this beautiful house with a huge wrap-around porch. I always wanted a porch like that, where you could sit out in the morning with a cup of coffee and just watch the neighborhood. My neighbor across the street has a porch, but he never uses it. He just leaves his old newspapers piled up on the steps. The paperboy just keeps throwing them right on top of the pile every morning. It's one of those kids who rides his bike and just tosses them without looking. When I was a kid, the paperboy actually walked up to the door and put it in the mailbox. People don't really do that anymore, do they? Everyone just reads the news on their phones now. I tried reading the news on my phone, but the screen is just so small, it hurts my eyes if I stare at it too long. I had to get reading glasses last year, these little tortoiseshell ones...\n\nWait, sorry. I lost my train of thought. But yeah, I just feel so guilty about my dad, and like I'm failing at everything.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific you did or didn't do for your father that you feel guilty about?\n\nPatient: Specific things? Um... well, I just... I constantly replay everything in my head. Like, I keep going over every single decision I made about his doctors' appointments, trying to figure out if I picked the wrong specialist or scheduled things too late. And then I think about every tense conversation I've had with my relatives about his care, how I snapped at them or didn't explain things right. \n\nMy aunt called last week, actually, the one who lives in Florida. She moved down there after she retired from the post office. She used to work at the main branch downtown, the one with the really heavy brass doors. They were always so hard to push open, especially in the winter when you had thick gloves on. I had this pair of leather gloves, they were lined with cashmere, and I lost one of them on the subway once. I was so upset because they were a gift from my old boss. He was a really nice guy, he always brought in donuts on Fridays from this little bakery near his house. They had the best crullers, you know, the twisted ones with the glaze? I tried making them at home once, but the dough just wouldn't fry right. It got all greasy and heavy. I think my oil wasn't hot enough. I need to get one of those candy thermometers that clips onto the side of the pot...\n\nOh, I'm sorry. I'm doing it again, aren't I? Um... but yeah, I just... I go over all those decisions and arguments, and I just end up feeling like I'm this incredibly selfish daughter. And honestly, it spills over into work too. I feel like a completely useless employee. I just feel like I deserve whatever bad things happen to me."
